Potential new algorithm: Simulated annealing on probabilities p with moves:
- Move a small mass from i to j (or j to i), updating q affected lags: changing p_i by δ affects q_{i+l} by δ(p_l + p_{i+l}+δ p_i?) Could update objective fast? n=8192, m maybe thousands, 2400s could do many if data structures. But FFT objective simpler.

We can do “coordinate descent” in log p: For coordinate i, maximizing? p_i affects many q_k quadratically. Could choose i with high gradient. But discrete moves can escape.

Another approach: Use “randomized coordinate projected subgradient” with reweighting: At each step, choose a few lags with high q and adjust p_i inversely to p_{k-i}. Mirror descent does this globally.

Mirror descent: p_i ← p_i exp(-η ∑_k w_k p_{k-i}) / Z. For active lag k, it reduces p_i in proportion to p_{k-i}; high-high pairs both reduced, mass redistributed to low partners. This is like multiplicative weights for pairwise constraints. Could be strong for sparse weighted Sidon. Let's emphasize.

We can implement alternating between:
- Mirror descent with weights w = top lags or LSE weights to reduce max.
- Periodic coordinate-wise “mass transfer” to fix infeasibility / explore.

Also use “restarts from random sparse seeds”.

Let's design an algorithm tailored to problem:

Given n (maybe 8192 or 16384). Let goal minimize objective = 2n max(p*p). Normalize p sum=1. We can work with probability p directly.

Helper functions top-level:
- ensure_short? no.
- conv_prob(p): full linear convolution via FFT (size next pow2 2n-1). Ensure real, maybe correct tiny negatives.
- objective(p): 2n * max(c) /? Since p sum=1, objective=2n*max_c. But if p sum not 1, use 2n*max_c/(sum p)^2. For final evaluate_sequence uses a not normalized; scale invariant. Use p.
- lse_obj(p, beta): c=conv(p); max_c; return logsumexp(beta(c-max_c)) + max_c? Optional.
- gradient_hankel(p, w): compute 2*H w p = 2∑_l p_l w_{i+l}. Need exact via FFT. Let's implement robust.

Compute H p for weight vector w length L=2n-1:
We need d_i = ∑_{l=0}^{n-1} p_l w_{i+l}, i=0..n-1.
Method: Use FFT of p and reversed w: br = w[::-1] length L. conv = ifft(fft(p, N)*fft(br, N))[:L+n-1] (linear conv length L+n-1 = 3n-2). d_i = conv[L-1-i]. Let's verify: conv[m]=∑_{l=0}^{n-1} p_l br[m-l] = ∑ p_l w[L-1-(m-l)] = ∑ p_l w[L-1-m+l]. Set L-1-m = i - l? Wait we want w_{i+l}. Set L-1-m+l = i+l => L-1-m = i => m = L-1-i. Then conv[L-1-i]=∑ p_l w_{i+l}. Good. So d=conv[L-1 down to L-n]. If using SCP? We'll implement with numpy fft. N next pow2 L+n-1 = 3n-2? For n=8192, L=16383, convlen=24574, N=32768. Optimizers:
A. Direct FISTA/prox with smoothed loss in probability space:

Let's design final algorithm pipeline:

1. Determine budget = 2350 (leave 50 for return/eval). array global? use time.monotonic.
2. Generate candidate length n=8192.
   - Load parent p0 = height_sequence_1 normalized to sum1 (nonnegative). If missing, use random smooth.
   - Create initial list:
     * p0, reverse(p0), (p0+rev)/2? rev same objective? But asym.
     * Power transforms p0**γ normalized for γ in [0.5,0.8,1,1.25,1.6,2].
     * Edge taper windows: p * (smooth ramp endpoints with strength e.g. 0, 0.3, 0.6). But if parent already.
     * Asymmetric multipliers: 1+α cos(2πx+φ) for α small [0.2,0.5], φ RANDOM, normalized, clip.
     * Four coming from convolve with small gaussian kernels (sharpen/deblur).
     * Sparse/random: random Dirichlet with concentration (α total maybe 10? support dense), random logarithmic normal? Also random “parent smoothed plus noise”.
   - Screen each with a fast local solver (e.g. 2-3s) and objective; keep top 8.
3. Main local optimization on top candidates using a robust “LSE-FISTA” or “L-BFGS” for some seconds each, maybe iteratively.
4. Basin hopping: mutate best and top population (Fourier/log perturbations) and polish short, updating best.
5. Final deep polish on global best:
   * Increase beta LSE FISTA / gradient with line search for some time.
   * Run log-domain L-BFGS with increasing beta (e.g. beta=100,1000,10000) for iterations until time left.
   * Run sparse coordinate descent / mirror descent to maybe lower.
6. Try larger n if time remains: derive from best p by upsampling (e.g. interpolate to 2n with monotone? or random perturb) and optimize quickly. But if no time, skip.

Let's implement `softmax(x)` top-level:
```
def softmax(x):
    x = x - np.max(x)
    y = np.exp(x)
    s=y.sum()
    if s<=0: return np.full(len(x),1/len(x))
    return y/s
```
For large n, subtract max ok.

Hankel mul implementation:
```
def hankel_mul(p, w):
    n=len(p); L=2*n-1
    br = w[::-1]
    size=1 << (L+n-1).bit_length()
    P=fft(p,size); W=fft(br,size)
    conv=np.real(ifft(P*W))
    return conv[L-1:L+n-1][::-1]
```
Length of conv should L+n-1 =3n-2. We need indices L-1 down to L-n inclusive. In Python, conv[L-1:L+n-1][::-1] gives length n? L-1 to L+n-2 inclusive length n. Good. Need check for n=2, w length3. But desired d0=∑_{l=0}^1 p_l w_{0+l}=p0w0+p1w1. Something off. Let's recalc with L=2n-1=3, w indices 0,1,2. Desired d0=w0 p0 + w1 p1. conv(a,br)[m]=∑ p_l br[m-l]=∑ p_l w[2-(m-l)] = ∑ p_l w[2-m+l]. Set 2-m=i? For i=0 => m=2. conv[2]=p0 w[2-2+0=0]+p1 w[2-2+1=1]=p0w0+p1w1. Good. conv length L+n-1=4 indices 0..3; m=2 okay. For i=1: m=1. conv[1]=p0 w[1]+p1 w[2]. desired p0w1+p1w2. Good. So d = conv[L-1 : L-1+n? l-1 to l-n inclusive reversed. For n=2: conv[2:4][::-1] gives conv[3],conv[2]; wrong. Need slice start L-n? Let's derive: m_i = L-1-i, for i=0..n-1 gives m from L-1 down to L-n. For L=3,n=2 -> m=2,1. So slice conv[L-n : L][?]. The values in increasing m: [L-n, ..., L-1] = conv[1:3] = m=1,2. Reverse -> [2,1]. So d = conv[L-n : L][::-1]. Good. General L-n = (2n-1)-n=n-1. Slice conv[n-1:2n-1]? length n. For n=2 conv[1:3]. Let's use `conv[n-1:2*n-1][::-1]` since 2n-1=L. Wait L=2n-1, L-n=n-1, L=2n-1. Slice end L (exclusive) length (2n-1)-(n-1)=n. Yes.

```

Let's develop a robust “hybrid BOBYQA” pipeline:

- Main iterative loop: alternate between:

Let's define `mix_conv(c_pp,c_pg,c_gg,t)` returns max maybe. For speed with multiple t, do arrays.
```
def mixture_max(c_pp,c_pg,c_gg,t_values):
    # arrays length L, t 1d
    # compute max over k and t: use broadcasting perhaps memory small 33*16383 ~1M.
    tt=t_values[:,None]
    val = (1-tt)**2*c_pp[None,:] + 2*tt*(1-tt)*c_pg[None,:] + tt*tt*c_gg[None,:]
    return val.max(axis=1)
```
Good.

def top_weights(c, K):
   L=len(c); K=min(K,L)
   idx=np.argpartition(c, -K)[-K:] # top K (unsorted)
   w=np.zeros(L); w[idx]=1.0/K
   return w
```

def projected_subgrad_step(p, grad, eta):

Let's design `run_vertex_search` more concretely.

```
def run_vertex_search(p0, deadline, rng, initial_obj=None, max_candidate=32, t_values=None, K_list=None):
    n=len(p0); p=normalize_prob(p0)
    if t_values is None: t_values=np.array([0.0,0.02,0.05,0.1,0.2,0.35,0.5,0.7,1.0], dtype=float)
    if K_list is None: K_list=[1,2,4,8,16,32,64,128]
    c=conv_self(p); obj=objective_prob(p,c)
    best_obj=obj; best_p=p.copy()
    it=0; stall=0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        if obj < best_obj:
            best_obj=obj; best_p=p.copy(); stall=0
        else:
            stall += 1
        if stall > 200: break? maybe continue with perturbation.
        c=conv_self(p) # maybe already
        # weights
        K = K_list[it % len(K_list)]
        w = top_lag_weights(c, K)
        h = hankel_mul(p,w)
        # candidate indices: bottom max_candidate of h, plus random candidates proportional to low h? 
        # Also if some h nan? use inf.
        finite = np.isfinite(h)
        h_safe=np.where(finite,h,1e100)
        # Use argpartition bottom
        mc = max(1, min(max_candidate, n))
        if mc == n: js = np.arange(n)
        else: js = np.argpartition(h_safe, mc-1)[:mc]
        # add random: maybe 4 random indices
        if n>mc: js = np.concatenate([js, rng.integers(0,n,4)])
        # maybe include indices where p is zero but h low? already in h.
        best_new_obj=obj; best_j=None; best_t=0.0; improved=False
        L=2*n-1
        for j in js:
            c_pg=np.zeros(L); c_pg[j:j+n]=p
            c_gg=np.zeros(L); c_gg[2*j]=1.0
            vals=mixture_max_values(c, c_pg, c_gg, t_values)
            idx=np.argmin(vals); v=vals[idx]; tv=t_values[idx]
            new_obj=2.0*n*v
            if new_obj < best_new_obj - 1e-12:
                best_new_obj=new_obj; best_j=int(j); best_t=float(tv); improved=True
                # optional if very small t, break? no
        if improved and best_j is not None:
            if best_t == 1.0:
                p=np.zeros(n); p[best_j]=1.0
            elif best_t > 0:
                p = (1.0-best_t)*p + best_t*np.zeros(n)? need set p[best_j]=1.
                new_p = p.copy(); new_p[best_j] = 1.0
                p = (1.0-best_t)*p + best_t*new_p
                p = normalize_prob(p)
            obj = best_new_obj
            # Recompute c? next loop does.
        else:
            # no improvement: take small additive gradient or mirror step
            # Use h as gradient; mirror small
            hmax = max(np.max(h),1e-12)
            eta = 0.05 / hmax
            newp = p*np.exp(-np.clip(eta*h, -10, 10))
            newp = normalize_prob(newp)
            # evaluate; if improves accept, else random small vertex
            c_tmp=conv_self(newp); obj_tmp=objective_prob(newp,c_tmp)
            if obj_tmp < obj:
                p=newp; obj=obj_tmp
            else:
                # random tiny perturbation: move small mass from random high p to random low h
                # choose i with high p, j low h
                if n>1:
                    i_idx=np.argmax(p)
                    # low h random among bottom maybe js
                    j_idx=js[rng.integers(len(js))]
                    r=1e-3
                    if p[i_idx] > r:
                        p = p.copy(); p[i_idx]-=r; p[j_idx]+=r; p=normalize_prob(p)
                        c=conv_self(p); obj=objective_prob(p,c)
                # elif recompute
                c=conv_self(p); obj=objective_prob(p,c)
        it +=1
    return best_p, best_obj
```

Maybe the best vertex move should be not adding p_j but transferring mass from some i to j, not moving all mass to j. Frank-Wolfe mixture (1-t)p+t e_j is a transfer of t mass to j, reducing all others proportionally. It is a coordinate move with global shrink. Could be too aggressive. A more natural move: p_new = p + t(e_j - e_i) for best pair (i,j). This changes two coordinates. We can evaluate quickly. This might be better and allow support growth without shrinking all. Let's add pair moves!

For a pair move: p_new=p; p_new[j]+=δ, p_new[i]-=δ, with 0≤δ≤p_i. This is a simplex translation. Frank-Wolfe vertex is equivalent to average over all i with δ weighted p_i? Actually (1-t)p+t e_j = p + t(e_j - p), moving t mass from every i. Pair move can be more local.
We can find best pair (i,j): linear active score h_i - h_j? For active W, change δ from i to j: first-order 2δ(h_j - h_i). Want h_j < h_i. Choose i with high p_i and high h_i? More specifically maximize p_i? Since δ limited by p_i, but objective may benefit large δ. For each candidate j low h, choose i = argmax h_i * p_i? Since convolution linear: p*Δ = p*e_j - p*e_i (but p*e_i and e_i*p both same? Convolution commutative, p*e_j = shift p). In full convolution, linear term = 2(p*e_j - p*e_i) because (p+δΔ)*(p+δΔ) includes p*δΔ + δΔ*p =2δ (p*Δ). So c_pg_j as for vertex (p shifted by j) and c_pg_i. Quadratic term = δ^2 (δ_{2j} - δ_{j+i} - δ_{j+i}? Wait Δ*Δ = e_j*e_j - e_j*e_i - e_i*e_j + e_i*e_i. Convolution commutative; e_j*e_i has mass 1 at j+i, and appears twice? Since (e_j-e_i)*(e_j-e_i)=e_j*e_j -2 e_j*e_i + e_i*e_i. In linear convolution, e_j*e_i has 1 at j+i. Thus quadratic coefficients: +1 at 2j, -2 at j+i, +1 at 2i. (Note i,j order). This is exact.
Then for δ in [0,p_i], c_new = A + 2δ(U_j - U_i) + δ^2 Q where U_j length L with p at j:j+n (if g vertex). Actually p*e_j = shift p by j (no factor). Linear term is 2δ( U_j - U_i ). Good.

def run_lbfgs_polish(p0, beta, maxiter=50):

    x=np.log(np.maximum(p,1e-12))
    # maybe center x by subtract mean to keep softmax near initial? softmax invariant to constant; to avoid huge x, subtract max.
    res=minimize(lbfgs_objgrad, x, method='L-BFGS-B', jac=True, args=(beta,), options={'maxiter':maxiter,'maxls':20,'ftol':1e-12,'gtol':1e-10})
    p_new=softmax(res.x)
    obj=...
    return p_new,obj
```

`lbfgs_objgrad`:
```
def lbfgs_objgrad(x, beta):
    n=len(x)
    x=x-np.max(x)
    e=np.exp(x); s=e.sum(); p=e/s
    c=conv_self(p)
    cm=c.max()
    scaled=beta*(c-cm)
    # scaled max 0; further subtract max for exp sum (0)
    # weights
    ws = np.exp(np.clip(scaled, -700, 700)) # scaled ≤0
    wsum=ws.sum()
    if wsum <=0: wsum=1; ws=np.zeros(len(c)); ws[int(np.argmax(c))]=1
    else: w=ws/wsum
    loss = cm + np.log(wsum)/beta
    g = 2.0*hankel_mul(p,w)
    g = g - np.dot(p,g)*p
    # if loss nan: return large, zeros
    return loss,g
```
Potential: beta large, scaled very negative; exp underflow okay. `hs`? Good.
But minimizing LSE loss may prefer p with many tiny values? okay.

Candidate generation:
```
def upsample_prob(p_in, n_target):
    n_in=len(p_in)
    if n_in == n_target: return normalize_prob(p_in)
    x_old=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,n_in)
    x_new=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,n_target)
    f = np.interp(x_new, x_old, p_in * n_in)
    p = f / n_target
    return normalize_prob(p)
```
Alternatively use `scipy.interpolate.interp1d` cubic? np.interp linear enough. Could add small noise.

```
def random_sparse_prob(n, rng, m, center=True):
   js = rng.choice(n, size=m, replace=False)
   w = rng.dirichlet(np.ones(m))
   p=np.zeros(n); p[js]=w; return p
```

Let's implement `run_local_refine` returning best and current.

Pseudo code with no closures:
```
def run_local_refine(p0, deadline, rng, max_iter=None):
    n=len(p0); p=normalize_prob(p0)
    c=conv_self(p); obj=objective_prob(p,c)
    best_p=p.copy(); best_obj=obj
    alloc_start=time.monotonic()
    total = max(1.0, deadline - alloc_start)
    it=0; stall=0
    t_vertex = np.array([0.005,0.01,0.02,0.05,0.1,0.2,0.35])
    while time.monotonic() < deadline - 1e-6:
        elapsed = time.monotonic()-alloc_start
        progress = min(1.0, elapsed/total)
        # recompute c,obj (p could be unchanged from previous mutation)
        c=conv_self(p); obj=objective_prob(p,c)
        if obj < best_obj - 1e-15:
            best_obj=obj; best_p=p.copy(); stall=0
        else:
            stall += 1
        # choose K
        Klist=[1,4,8,16,32,64,128]
        K=Klist[it % len(Klist)] if it < len(Klist) else Klist[-1]
        w=top_lag_weights(c,K)
        h=hankel_mul(p,w)
        # candidate counts
        low_count=16 if progress<0.5 else 28
        high_count=10 if progress<0.5 else 16
        # low js
        h_safe=np.where(np.isfinite(h),h,1e100)
        low_js=np.argpartition(h_safe, min(low_count,n)-1)[:low_count] if n>low_count else np.arange(n)
        # high is
        if n>1:
            score=i? `score = p*h_safe`
            high_idx=np.argpartition(-score, min(high_count,n)-1)[:high_count]
            # ensure argmax p and high h included
            high_js=np.unique(np.concatenate([high_idx, np.array([np.argmax(p), np.argmax(h_safe)])]))
        else: high_js=np.array([0])
        # evaluate moves
        best_move_obj=obj+1e-15; kind=0; args=None
        # pair moves
        # build candidate union
        cand = np.unique(np.concatenate([low_js, high_js]))
        # if len too large, limit? cand size <= low+high+2 ~50
        shifts = {}
        for idx in cand:
            arr=np.zeros(2*n-1, dtype=float); arr[int(idx):int(idx)+n]=p; shifts[int(idx)]=arr
        deltas = [0.02,0.05,0.1,0.2,0.35,0.6,0.9,1.0]
        for j in low_js:
            for i in high_js:
                if i==j: continue
                pi=p[i]
                if pi <= 1e-14: continue
                Udiff = shifts[int(j)] - shifts[int(i)]
                # deltas scaled by pi
                for dfrac in deltas:
                    d = dfrac*pi
                    if d <= 1e-15: continue
                    v = c + (2.0*d)*Udiff
                    d2=d*d
                    v[2*j] += d2
                    v[2*i] += d2
                    v[j+i] -= 2.0*d2
                    m = v.max()
                    newobj = 2.0*n*m
                    if newobj < best_move_obj - 1e-15:
                        best_move_obj=newobj; kind=1; args=(i,j,d)
                if time.monotonic() > deadline - 0.2: break
            if time.monotonic() > deadline - 0.2: break
        # vertex moves for low_js top vertex_count
        vertex_count = 4
        for j in low_js[:vertex_count]:
            c_pg = shifts[int(j)]
            c_gg = np.zeros(2*n-1); c_gg[2*j]=1.0
            for tv in t_vertex:
                v = (1-tv)*(1-tv)*c + 2*tv*(1-tv)*c_pg + tv*tv*c_gg
                newobj = 2.0*n*v.max()
                if newobj < best_move_obj - 1e-15:
                    best_move_obj=newobj; kind=2; args=(j,tv)
        if kind==1:
            i,j,d=args
            p=p.copy(); p[i]-=d; p[j]+=d; p=normalize_prob(p); obj=best_move_obj
            stall=0
        elif kind==2:
            j,tv=args
            p=np.zeros(n); p[j]=1.0; p=(1-tv)*p_old + tv*p? Need old p before overwrite. Save p_old.
        else:
            # no improvement: small operations
            ...
        it+=1
    return best_p,best_obj
```
Need be careful when using `shifts` arrays and modifying v modifies shifts? `v = c + (2d)*Udiff` creates new array; modifying v doesn't affect shifts. Good.
But `v = c + ...` alloc; if we modify v, okay.
In vertex, `v` new array; modifying c_gg no.

p=normalize_prob(newp)
```
Actually mixture (1-t)p + t e_j: newp=(1-t)*p_old; newp[j]+=t. Good.

For pair update: if d > p_i due floating, clip; normalize.

    hmax=max(np.max(h_safe),1e-12)
    eta = 0.1 / hmax
    newp = p * np.exp(np.clip(-eta*h_safe, -5, 5))
    newp = normalize_prob(newp)
    c_tmp=conv_self(newp); obj_tmp=objective_prob(newp,c_tmp)
    if obj_tmp < obj:

    else:

       if n>1:
          i= int(high_js[rng.integers(len(high_js))]); j=int(low_js[rng.integers(len(low_js))]);
          if i!=j and p[i]>1e-9:
             d=1e-4*p[i]; p=p.copy(); p[i]-=d; p[j]+=d; p=normalize_prob(p); obj=objective...

          scale=0.03+0.1*min(stall/100,1.0)
          p=fourier_mutate_prob(base, rng, scale)
          obj=objective...; stall=0
```

Now, `top_lag_weights(c,K)`: If K=1, returns one-hot max. Argpartition for K=L. Use:
```
def top_lag_weights(c, K):
    L=len(c); K=max(1,min(K,L))
    if K==L:
       return np.ones(L)/L
    idx=np.argpartition(c, -K)[-K:]
    w=np.zeros(L); w[idx]=1.0/K; return w
```
Could weight by c values: maybe uniform. Good.

But for K=1, h is shift for max lag. For pair search, moving mass from high i to low j based on h may not reduce max if current max at k: h_i = p_{k-i}, h_j = p_{k-j}. If p_i high? High score for source i should be p_i * h_i = p_i p_{k-i}. This is exactly the contribution of pair (i,k-i) to max? Partially. Good.

Implementation details:

### `normalize_prob`
```
def normalize_prob(vec):
    v = np.asarray(vec, dtype=np.float64)
    v = np.where(v > 0.0, v, 0.0)
    s = v.sum()
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s < 1e-300:
        return np.full(len(v), 1.0 / len(v), dtype=np.float64)
    return v / s
```
Could `np.nan_to_num`? If input NaN, set 0. Use `np.isfinite`.
```
v = np.nan_to_num(v, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
```
Then clip. Good.

### `conv_self`
Use scipy.fft; for small n? `size = 1 << (2*n-1).bit_length()`. If n=0 error. `p = np.asarray` no copy maybe. Use `fft(p, size)`. Real.
```
def conv_self(vec):
    a=np.asarray(vec,dtype=np.float64)
    n=a.size
    size=1 << (2*n-1).bit_length()
    A=fft(a, size)
    c=np.real(ifft(A*A))
    c=c[:2*n-1]
    # remove tiny imaginary already; clip negative? Maybe max unaffected, but for formulas with linear add, small negative could affect max? Max likely positive. But pair move exact can produce tiny negative at some lags; max positive. Use `c = np.maximum(c, 0.0)` to ensure nonnegative. But for LSE gradient, clipping changes derivative near zero; okay. For true objective, clip okay. For pair formula, base c clipped loses tiny negative but base exact nonnegative; okay.
    return c
```
Clipping inside objective reduces maybe if true c max negative? impossible since c_0>=0. Good.

Note: For pair formula, if c has clipped negatives to 0 and Udiff linear, resulting v may be slightly different from true if base near 0; negligible. Could avoid clipping in conv_self and only clip in objective? But formula exact with true c may have tiny negative; max positive. It's okay. Let's not clip in conv_self, to keep formulas exact. But LSE weights with tiny negative? okay. Objective max unaffected. However if due FFT roundoff c has small negative large? not.
```
c = np.real_if_close(...)
c[np.abs(c)<1e-15]=0.0? maybe.
```
Keep as real.

### `objective_prob`
```
def objective_prob(vec, c=None):
   v=np.asarray(vec)
   if c is None: c=conv_self(v)
   s=v.sum()
   if s<1e-300: return np.inf
   m=float(np.max(c))
   if not np.isfinite(m): return np.inf
   return 2.0*len(v)*m/(s*s)
```
If c contains tiny negative, max positive.

### `project_simplex`
Use previous. Need S maybe float. For p sum=1. Ensure nonnegative.
```
def project_simplex(v, S):
    n=len(v); v=np.asarray(v,dtype=np.float64)
    w=np.sort(v)[::-1]
    cs=np.cumsum(w)
    rho=np.arange(1,n+1,dtype=np.float64)
    cond = (cs - S)/rho <= w
    if not np.any(cond): arr=np.zeros(n); return arr
    ridx=int(np.max(np.where(cond)[0]))
    theta=(cs[ridx]-S)/(ridx+1)
    return np.maximum(v-theta,0.0)
```
Wait previous formula: `rho = max idx+1` where idx 0-based; theta=(cumsum[rho-1]-S)/rho. Let's implement carefully.
Sort descending w[0..n-1]. For candidate first m, theta_m=(sum_{0:m-1}w - S)/m. Need largest m where theta_m <= w[m-1]. So compute `sum_w = np.cumsum(w)`, `m_range = np.arange(1,n+1)`, `theta = (sum_w - S)/m_range`, condition theta <= w[m-1] (with w index m-1). If none, rho=n? Actually for last m=n, theta=(sum_v-S)/n. If all v<S condition? There is always m=n? If v.sum() maybe >S, condition may fail all? Example v all 0,S=1 -> theta=-1/n <= w? condition true. So any. Use `valid = theta <= w[:-?` Need align. `theta[m-1] <= w[m-1]`. Good. `ridx = np.max(np.where(valid)[0])` 0-based; m=ridx+1; theta_m. Return max(v-theta,0). Good. Use `if not valid any` fallback. But any false unlikely.

### `hankel_mul`
```
def hankel_mul(p, w):
   p=np.asarray(p,float); w=np.asarray(w,float)
   n=p.size; L=w.size
   if L != 2*n-1: # maybe if w different? pad/truncate
   br = w[::-1]
   size=1 << (L + n - 1).bit_length()
   P=fft(p,size); W=fft(br,size)
   conv=np.real(ifft(P*W))
   return conv[n-1:2*n-1][::-1]
```
Check slice: size maybe L+n-1; conv length enough. For n=1, L+n-1=1, slice conv[0:1] ok.

Potential performance: `w[::-1]` view. FFT of w every iteration. Could compute h via direct if K small? For K=1, direct faster. We can add a fast path for sparse weights? `top_lag_weights` returns dense. We can have a separate `hankel_top_shifts(p, idx)` for K small? But FFT okay. For n=8192, FFT size 32768 each iteration, maybe 0.001s. Good.

### `top_lag_weights`
Use argpartition. If c has NaN? no.

### `shift_p_array(p,j)`
```
def shift_p_array(p,j):
   n=len(p); arr=np.zeros(2*n-1); arr[j:j+n]=p; return arr
```
For many indices, loop.

### `fourier_mutate_prob`
```
def fourier_mutate_prob(p, rng, scale):
   n=len(p); fsize=1 << (2*n-1).bit_length()
   A=fft(p, fsize)
   mag=np.abs(A); phase=np.angle(A)
   freqs=np.arange(fsize, dtype=float)
   # weight maybe 1 + 2*freqs/fsize
   noise = rng.normal(0.0, scale*(1.0 + freqs/fsize), fsize)
   newA = mag*np.exp(1j*(phase+noise))
   newA[0]=A[0]
   newp=np.real(ifft(newA, fsize))[:n]
   return project_simplex(newp, 1.0)
```
If scale=0, return p copy.

### `random_sparse_prob`
```
def random_sparse_prob(n, rng, alpha):
   m=int(round(alpha*np.sqrt(n)))
   m=max(2,min(n,m))
   js=rng.choice(n, size=m, replace=False)
   w=rng.dirichlet(np.ones(m))
   p=np.zeros(n); p[js]=w; return p
```
Could use different alpha [1,2,3,5,8]. Position random. Might be high objective but okay.

### `upsample_prob`
As above. If n_in <2? handle.

### `make_transformed_parent(p, rng, transform_id)` maybe. We'll just create list in propose with loops.

Now, `run_local_refine` implementation with time and parameters.

Need avoid very large memory from `shifts` dict if many candidates. Use dict `shift_map = {}` top-level? Inside function local variable okay (not closure). Rule no closures; local variables fine.
```
def run_local_refine(p0, deadline, rng, low_count=16, high_count=10, vertex_count=4, delta_frac=None):
```
But no default arrays? We can define inside from globals? Use top-level constants? We can define global constants `GLOBAL_DELTA_FRACS = np.array([...])`? But arrays created at import. Fine. Or pass None and create inside. No lambda. Use local arrays.

Pseudo code with time management inside:
```
def run_local_refine(p0, deadline, rng):
    p=normalize_prob(p0)
    n=p.size
    c=conv_self(p)
    obj=objective_prob(p,c)
    best_p=p.copy(); best_obj=obj
    delta_fr=np.array([0.01,0.03,0.08,0.18,0.35,0.6,1.0])
    t_vert=np.array([0.01,0.02,0.05,0.1,0.2,0.4])
    Klist=np.array([1,2,4,8,16,32,64,128,256])
    it=0; stall=0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        elapsed = time.monotonic()-start
        progress = min(1.0, elapsed / max(1.0, deadline-start))
        c = conv_self(p)
        obj = objective_prob(p,c)
        if obj < best_obj - 1e-14:
            best_obj=obj; best_p=p.copy(); stall=0
        else:
            stall += 1
        # If remaining very small break?
        # weights
        K = int(Klist[it % len(Klist)])
        w = top_lag_weights(c,K)
        h = hankel_mul(p,w)
        h = np.where(np.isfinite(h), h, 1e100)
        h = np.maximum(h, 0.0)
        # low candidates
        lc = 12 if progress<0.3 else (20 if progress<0.7 else 28)
        lc = max(1, min(lc, n))
        if lc < n:
             low_js = np.argpartition(h, lc-1)[:lc]
        else: low_js = np.arange(n)
        # high candidates
        score = p * h
        hc = 8 if progress<0.3 else (14 if progress<0.7 else 20)
        hc = max(1,min(hc,n))
        if hc < n:
             high_js = np.argpartition(-score, hc-1)[:hc]
        else: high_js=np.arange(n)
        # include some deterministic
        if n > 0:
            high_js = np.unique(np.concatenate((high_js, np.array([np.argmax(p), np.argmax(h)]))))
            low_js = np.unique(np.concatenate((low_js, np.array([np.argmin(h)]))))
        best_obj_move = obj
        best_kind=0; bestArgs=None
        # Pair moves
        # To limit candidate union, if unique > 60, trim low to 24 high 12? But okay.
        cand = np.unique(np.concatenate((low_js, high_js)))
        L=2*n-1
        shift_map={}
        for idx in cand:
            ii=int(idx); arr=np.zeros(L); arr[ii:ii+n]=p; shift_map[ii]=arr
        # Pair loops
        # For speed, maybe skip pairs if n > 20000? still okay.
        # Use local variables
        for jj in low_js:
            j=int(jj)
            Uj=shift_map[j]
            for ii in high_js:
                i=int(ii)
                if i==j: continue
                pi=float(p[i])
                if pi <= 1e-15: continue
                Udiff=Uj - shift_map[i]
                # deltas
                for df in delta_fr:
                    d=df*pi
                    if d <= 1e-18: continue
                    v=c + (2.0*d)*Udiff
                    d2=d*d
                    v[2*j] += d2
                    v[2*i] += d2
                    idx_q=j+i
                    v[idx_q] -= 2.0*d2
                    mn=float(v.max())
                    nov=2.0*n*mn
                    if nov < best_obj_move - 1e-14:
                        best_obj_move=nov; best_kind=1; bestArgs=(i,j,d)
                if time.monotonic() > deadline - 0.5:
                    break
            if time.monotonic() > deadline - 0.5:
                break
        # Vertex moves only if time left
        if time.monotonic() < deadline - 0.5:
            vc = 3 if progress<0.5 else 5
            for jj in low_js[:vc]:
                j=int(jj); Uj=shift_map[j]
                cgg=np.zeros(L); cgg[2*j]=1.0
                for tv in t_vert:
                    v=(1.0-tv)*(1.0-tv)*c + (2.0*tv*(1.0-tv))*Uj + (tv*tv)*cgg
                    mn=float(v.max()); nov=2.0*n*mn
                    if nov < best_obj_move - 1e-14:
                        best_obj_move=nov; best_kind=2; bestArgs=(j,tv)
        if best_kind == 1:
            i,j,d=bestArgs
            p=p.copy()
            if d > p[i]: d=p[i]
            p[i]-=d; p[j]+=d
            p=normalize_prob(p)
            obj=best_obj_move
            stall=0
        elif best_kind == 2:
            j,tv=bestArgs
            old=p.copy()
            p=(1.0-tv)*old
            p[j] += tv
            p=normalize_prob(p)
            obj=best_obj_move
            stall=0
        else:
            # fallback
            # mirror small
            hmax=float(np.max(h)) if h.size else 1.0
            if hmax > 1e-300:
                eta=0.15/hmax
                newp=p*np.exp(np.clip(-eta*h, -6.0, 6.0))
                newp=normalize_prob(newp)
                c_chk=conv_self(newp); o_chk=objective_prob(newp,c_chk)
                if o_chk < obj:
                    p=newp; obj=o_chk; stall=0
                else:
                    # random tiny pair
                    if n>1:
                        i_idx=int(high_js[rng.integers(len(high_js))])
                        j_idx=int(low_js[rng.integers(len(low_js))])
                        if i_idx != j_idx and p[i_idx] > 1e-12:
                            ddel=1e-4*float(p[i_idx])
                            p=p.copy(); p[i_idx]-=ddel; p[j_idx]+=ddel; p=normalize_prob(p)
                            c=conv_self(p); obj=objective_prob(p,c)
                    if stall >= 30:
                        base = best_p if rng.random() < 0.5 else p
                        scale = 0.03 + 0.07*min(1.0, stall/100.0)
                        p = fourier_mutate_prob(base, rng, scale)
                        c=conv_self(p); obj=objective_prob(p,c)
                        stall=0
            else:
                # uniform?
                if stall >= 20:
                    p = rng.dirichlet(np.ones(n))
                    c=conv_self(p); obj=objective_prob(p,c); stall=0
        it += 1
        if it % 50 == 0:
            print("   local it=%d obj=%.8f best=%.8f" % (it,obj,best_obj))
    # final evaluate best
    return best_p, best_obj
```
Potential issue: `obj=best_obj_move` after move but due normalization/roundoff actual objective maybe slightly different; recompute next loop. Good.

But in no improvement mirror, we recompute c and obj; if after mutating stall=0. If p changed but not improved, next loop recompute and stall increments. Good.

Could there be infinite loops if no move and stall<30, random tiny pair doesn't improve but p changes, loop continues. Fine.

We can do a timed warmup? Instead use small maxiter and only call when deadline - time > 60. 20 iterations likely ~5s for n=8192. Good.

`run_lbfgs_polish`:
```
def run_lbfgs_polish(p0, beta, maxiter=20, maxls=8):
   p=normalize_prob(p0)
   x=np.log(np.maximum(p, 1e-300))
   x = x - np.max(x)
   try:
       res=minimize(lbfgs_fun, x, method='L-BFGS-B', jac=True, args=(beta,), options={'maxiter':maxiter,'maxls':maxls,'ftol':1e-14,'gtol':1e-10,'maxcor':8,'disp':False})
       p_new=softmax(res.x)
   except Exception:
       p_new=p.copy()
   return p_new
```
No objective return; caller evaluates. Could if `res.x` invalid, fallback.

`softmax` top-level:
```
def softmax(x):
   x=np.asarray(x,float); x=x-np.max(x); e=np.exp(x); s=e.sum(); if s<1e-300: return uniform; return e/s
```
In `lbfgs_fun`, use stable loss. Need ensure g finite. If not, return 1e100 zeros.

Potential problem: L-BFGS may increase LSE by changing p to more uniform? But we keep best.

Now, `coarse_start` phase: We can use `run_local_refine` on coarse n. But it uses project_simplex and pair search; okay. Since n small, faster. We'll upsample parent to coarse. Add candidate.

Time allocation in propose:
```
def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400):
    total_start=time.monotonic(); deadline=total_start+min(budget_s,2400)-30
    rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
    best_p=None; best_obj=np.inf
    # load parent
    p_full = None
    try: p_full=normalize_prob(height_sequence_1)
    except: p_full=None
    n_target = 8192
    # If parent length not n, still? Use given length? Maybe keep parent length. But we want n_target. If parent length !=8192, use parent length as n_target? It says length construction 8192. Use parent length if available else 8192.
    if p_full is not None and len(p_full)>=64:
        n_target=len(p_full)
    else: n_target=8192
```
But if parent length 8192, good. If we want fixed 8192 even if parent different? Use parent length to leverage. But if parent length e.g. 16384? Use it. Good.

If parent missing, generate fallback (e.g. `p = np.maximum(np.sin(np.pi*x)**2, 1e-4) normalized`).

But if parent length not 8192 and target maybe 8192? Hard. We'll use parent length.

Need candidate list of full p length n_target.
- If p_full: append.
- Reversed: rev, symmetric avg? Append.
- Power transforms: gamma list [0.4,0.6,0.8,1.0,1.25,1.6,2.2] -> p^gamma normalized.
- Asymmetric cosine: for alpha [0.2,0.4], phi [0,π/2,π,3π/2], p*max(1+alpha*sin/cos,1e-6) normalized.

Let's write functions carefully.

`normalize_prob`:
```
def normalize_prob(vec):
    v = np.asarray(vec, dtype=np.float64)
    v = np.nan_to_num(v, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    v = np.where(v > 0.0, v, 0.0)
    s = float(v.sum())
    if s < 1e-300:
        return np.full(v.shape, 1.0 / v.size, dtype=np.float64)
    return v / s
```
If v.size=0? Not; but guard:
```
if v.size ==0: return np.zeros(0)
```
But no empty. Add.

`conv_self`:
```
def conv_self(vec):
    a = np.asarray(vec, dtype=np.float64)
    n = a.size
    if n == 0: return np.zeros(0)
    size = 1 << (2*n - 1).bit_length()
    A = fft(a, size)
    c = np.real(ifft(A * A))
    c = c[:2*n-1]
    # clean tiny
    # c[np.abs(c) < 1e-15] = 0.0 maybe
    return c
```
If FFT produces -1e-18, max unaffected. For pair formula, negative tiny okay. Maybe set to 0 for stability: `c = np.where(c > 0.0, c, 0.0)`? This alters exact but can prevent negative max? Max positive. I will not clip in conv_self, but in objective max if all negative due tiny? impossible. Use `return c`.

`objective_prob`:
```
def objective_prob(vec, c=None):
    v=np.asarray(vec)
    if c is None: c=conv_self(v)
    s=float(np.sum(v)); n=v.size
    if s < 1e-300: return np.inf
    m=float(np.max(c))
    if not np.isfinite(m): return np.inf
    return float(2.0 * n * m / (s*s))
```
If m negative? objective negative? Max should nonneg. Could max(0,m). Use `m = max(0.0, m)`.

`project_simplex` as above. If S negative? not.

`hankel_mul`:
```
def hankel_mul(p, w):
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64); w=np.asarray(w,dtype=np.float64)
    n=p.size
    L = 2*n - 1
    if w.size != L:
        # pad/truncate
        tmp=np.zeros(L); m=min(L,w.size); tmp[:m]=w[:m]; w=tmp
    br = w[::-1]
    size = 1 << (L + n - 1).bit_length()
    P = fft(p, size); W = fft(br, size)
    conv = np.real(ifft(P * W))
    return conv[n-1:2*n-1][::-1]
```
For n=1, L+n-1=1, slice conv[0:1] ok.

`top_lag_weights`:
```
def top_lag_weights(c, K):
    L = len(c); K=int(max(1,min(K,L)))
    if K == L:
        w = np.ones(L, dtype=np.float64); w /= L; return w
    idx = np.argpartition(c, -K)[-K:]
    w = np.zeros(L, dtype=np.float64)
    w[idx] = 1.0 / K
    return w
```
Could if c has nan? no.

`fourier_mutate_prob`: as above. `project_simplex` returns sum S but due numerical may not exactly 1? It does; normalize maybe. Add `return normalize_prob(project_simplex(...))`.

`random_sparse_prob`: if n=1 return [1].

`upsample_prob`:
```
def upsample_prob(p_in, n_target):
    p=normalize_prob(p_in); n_in=len(p)
    if n_in == n_target: return p.copy()
    if n_in <= 1:
       return np.full(n_target, 1.0/n_target)
    x_old = np.linspace(0.0,1.0,n_in)
    x_new = np.linspace(0.0,1.0,n_target)
    f = np.interp(x_new, x_old, p * n_in)
    p_new = f / n_target
    return normalize_prob(p_new)
```
This may not preserve exact endpoint masses? okay. Could use `np.maximum(f,0)`. interp nonneg if p nonneg.

`softmax` as above.

`lbfgs_fun`:
```
def lbfgs_fun(x, beta):
    x = np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float64)
    n = x.size
    if n == 0: return 0.0, np.zeros(0)
    xm = np.max(x)
    e = np.exp(x - xm)
    s = e.sum()
    if s < 1e-300:
       p = np.full(n, 1.0/n); g=np.zeros(n); return 1e100,g
    p = e/s
    c = conv_self(p)
    cm = float(np.max(c))
    if not np.isfinite(cm): return 1e100, np.zeros(n)
    scaled = beta * (c - cm)
    scaled = np.minimum(scaled, 0.0)
    # exp safe: scaled <=0, but could be -inf
    ws = np.exp(scaled)
    wsum = float(ws.sum())
    if wsum < 1e-300:
       w = np.zeros_like(c); w[int(np.argmax(c))] = 1.0
    else:
       w = ws / wsum
    loss = cm + np.log(wsum) / beta if wsum > 0 else cm
    g = 2.0 * hankel_mul(p, w)
    pg = float(np.dot(p, g))
    g = g - pg * p
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(g)):
       return 1e100, np.zeros(n)
    if not np.isfinite(loss): loss=1e100
    return float(loss), g
```
If beta=0? avoid; pass >0.

`run_lbfgs_polish`:
```
def run_lbfgs_polish(p0, beta, maxiter=20, maxls=8):
    p = normalize_prob(p0)
    n = p.size
    x = np.log(np.maximum(p, 1e-300))
    x = x - np.max(x)
    try:
        res = minimize(lbfgs_fun, x, method='L-BFGS-B', jac=True, args=(beta,),
                       options={'maxiter': int(maxiter), 'maxls': int(maxls),
                                'ftol': 1e-14, 'gtol': 1e-10, 'maxcor': 12, 'disp': False})
        p_new = softmax(res.x)
    except Exception:
        p_new = p.copy()
    return normalize_prob(p_new)
```

`run_local_refine` with buffers. Need be careful with `np.argpartition` when K=0. We'll set.

Let's write `run_local_refine` fully:

```
def run_local_refine(p0, deadline, rng):
    p = normalize_prob(p0)
    n = p.size
    if n == 0:
       return p, np.inf
    start = time.monotonic()
    if start >= deadline:
       c=conv_self(p); return p, objective_prob(p,c)
    c = conv_self(p); obj = objective_prob(p, c)
    best_p = p.copy(); best_obj = obj
    delta_fr = np.array([0.005, 0.015, 0.04, 0.09, 0.18, 0.35, 0.65], dtype=np.float64)
    t_vert = np.array([0.005, 0.015, 0.03, 0.06, 0.12, 0.25, 0.5], dtype=np.float64)
    Klist = [1,2,4,8,16,32,64,128,256]
    L = 2*n -1
    Ubase_buf = np.empty(L, dtype=np.float64)
    v_buf = np.empty(L, dtype=np.float64)
    cgg_buf = np.empty(L, dtype=np.float64)
    it=0; stall=0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        elapsed = time.monotonic() - start
        progress = min(1.0, elapsed / max(1.0, deadline-start))
        c = conv_self(p); obj = objective_prob(p,c)
        if obj < best_obj - 1e-13:
            best_obj = obj; best_p = p.copy(); stall = 0
        else:
            stall += 1
        # if very close to deadline, break
        if time.monotonic() > deadline - 0.05:
            break
        K = Klist[it % len(Klist)]
        w = top_lag_weights(c, K)
        h = hankel_mul(p, w)
        h = np.where(np.isfinite(h), h, 1.0)
        h = np.maximum(h, 0.0)
        # low candidates
        if progress < 0.3:
            lc = 10
        elif progress < 0.7:
            lc = 18
        else:
            lc = 26
        lc = max(1, min(lc, n))
        if lc < n:
            low_js = np.argpartition(h, lc - 1)[:lc]
        else:
            low_js = np.arange(n)
        score = p * h
        if progress < 0.3:
            hc = 8
        elif progress < 0.7:
            hc = 14
        else:
            hc = 20
        hc = max(1, min(hc, n))
        if hc < n:
            high_js = np.argpartition(-score, hc - 1)[:hc]
        else:
            high_js = np.arange(n)
        if n > 0:
            high_js = np.unique(np.concatenate((high_js, np.array([np.argmax(p), np.argmax(h)], dtype=np.int64))))
            low_js = np.unique(np.concatenate((low_js, np.array([np.argmin(h)], dtype=np.int64))))
            low_js = low_js[:min(40, n)]
            high_js = high_js[:min(30, n)]
        best_move_obj = obj
        best_kind = 0
        best_i = -1; best_j = -1; best_d = 0.0; best_tv = 0.0
        # build shifts for candidates
        cand = np.unique(np.concatenate((low_js, high_js)))
        if n > 1 and cand.size > 0:
            # Preallocate a dict of shifts for cand. For performance, maybe list dict.
            shift_map = {}
            for idx in cand:
                ii = int(idx)
                arr = np.zeros(L, dtype=np.float64)
                arr[ii:ii+n] = p
                shift_map[ii] = arr
            # Pair loops
            for jj in low_js:
                j = int(jj)
                Uj = shift_map.get(j)
                if Uj is None: continue
                for ii in high_js:
                    i = int(ii)
                    if i == j: continue
                    pi = float(p[i])
                    if pi <= 1e-15: continue
                    U_i = shift_map.get(i)
                    if U_i is None: continue
                    np.subtract(Uj, U_i, out=Ubase_buf)
                    for dfrac in delta_fr:
                        d = float(dfrac) * pi
                        if d <= 1e-18: continue
                        scale = 2.0 * d
                        np.multiply(Ubase_buf, scale, out=v_buf)
                        np.add(c, v_buf, out=v_buf)
                        d2 = d * d
                        idx1 = 2*j
                        idx2 = 2*i
                        idxq = j + i
                        v_buf[idx1] = v_buf[idx1] + d2
                        v_buf[idx2] = v_buf[idx2] + d2
                        v_buf[idxq] = v_buf[idxq] - 2.0 * d2
                        nov = 2.0 * n * float(v_buf.max())
                        if nov < best_move_obj - 1e-13:
                            best_move_obj = nov; best_kind = 1
                            best_i = i; best_j = j; best_d = d
                    if time.monotonic() > deadline - 0.2:
                        break
                if time.monotonic() > deadline - 0.2:
                    break
            # Vertex moves
            if time.monotonic() < deadline - 0.2:
                vc = 6 if progress < 0.5 else 10
                for jj in low_js[:vc]:
                    j = int(jj)
                    Uj = shift_map.get(j)
                    if Uj is None: continue
                    cgg = np.zeros(L, dtype=np.float64)
                    cgg[2*j] = 1.0
                    for tv in t_vert:
                        tv = float(tv)
                        a1 = 1.0 - tv
                        np.multiply(Uj, a1*a1, out=v_buf) # reuse v_buf? Need base c part too.
                        # easier: v_buf = c*a1^2 + Uj*2tv(1-tv)+cgg*tv^2
                        # We can do:
                        np.multiply(c, a1*a1, out=v_buf)
                        np.multiply(Uj, 2.0*tv*a1, out=cgg_buf)
                        v_buf += cgg_buf
                        np.multiply(cgg, tv*tv, out=cgg_buf)
                        v_buf += cgg_buf
                        nov = 2.0 * n * float(v_buf.max())
                        if nov < best_move_obj - 1e-13:
                            best_move_obj = nov; best_kind = 2
                            best_j = j; best_tv = tv
            # apply if improvement
            if best_kind == 1:
                d = best_d
                if d > p[best_i]: d = p[best_i]
                p = p.copy()
                p[best_i] -= d
                p[best_j] += d
                p = normalize_prob(p)
                obj = best_move_obj
                stall = 0
            elif best_kind == 2:
                old = p.copy()
                p = (1.0 - best_tv) * old
                p[best_j] += best_tv
                p = normalize_prob(p)
                obj = best_move_obj
                stall = 0
            else:
                # fallback operations
                hmax = float(np.max(h)) if h.size else 1.0
                improved_fallback = False
                if hmax > 1e-300:
                    for eta in (0.1, 0.25, 0.5, 1.0, 2.0):
                        newp = p * np.exp(np.clip(-eta * h / hmax, -12.0, 12.0))
                        newp = normalize_prob(newp)
                        c_chk = conv_self(newp)
                        o_chk = objective_prob(newp, c_chk)
                        if o_chk < obj - 1e-14:
                            p = newp; obj = o_chk; c = c_chk
                            improved_fallback = True
                            stall = 0
                            break
                if not improved_fallback:
                    # small log noise
                    for sigma in (0.05, 0.15, 0.35):
                        newp = p * np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, sigma, n))
                        newp = normalize_prob(newp)
                        c_chk = conv_self(newp)
                        o_chk = objective_prob(newp, c_chk)
                        if o_chk < obj:
                            p = newp; obj = o_chk; c = c_chk
                            stall = 0
                            break
                    if stall >= 30:
                        base = best_p if rng.random() < 0.5 else p
                        scale = 0.04 + 0.10 * min(1.0, stall / 100.0)
                        p = fourier_mutate_prob(base, rng, scale)
                        c = conv_self(p); obj = objective_prob(p, c)
                        stall = 0
        it += 1
        if it % 25 == 0:
            print("     local it=%d obj=%.8f best=%.8f" % (it, obj, best_obj))
    # final update
    c = conv_self(best_p); objb = objective_prob(best_p, c)
    if objb < best_obj: best_obj=objb
    return best_p, best_obj
```
Note: In vertex computation, I reused `v_buf` multiple times: after `np.multiply(c, a1*a1, out=v_buf)`, then `np.multiply(Uj, factor, out=cgg_buf)`, `v_buf += cgg_buf`, then cgg. Good. But `cgg` dense zeros each vertex. Could allocate cgg_buf and set. Use `cgg = np.zeros(L)` each v; small. Fine.
Potential issue: `v_buf` was used in pair loops and may contain old; overwritten by multiply c. Good.
`cgg_buf` length L; okay.
`best_i` etc. Need reset? not necessary.

In fallback, after mirror update, we set `c=c_chk` but next loop recomputes. Good.

Fine point: `best_p` may be updated in loop, but `base=best_p` in mutation might be copying? fourier returns copy. Good.

Let's test mentally L-BFGS function gradient sign. We minimize L=(1/beta)lse. g_p=2H_w p. For p>0, moving x to increase p_j where g_j lower. L-BFGS okay.

Potential bug: `hankel_mul` returns H_w p = ∑ p_l w_{i+l}. For LSE loss derivative =2H_w p. Yes.

Let's verify `hankel_mul` formula with w one-hot k. n=2, w_k. Desired d0=p_{k}. For k=1: d0=p1? Formula conv[n-1:2n-1][::-1]. p=[p0,p1], w=[0,1,0]. br=[0,1,0]. conv length4? p*br: index0 0,1 p0*? Let's compute: conv = [0, p0, p1,0]? slice conv[1:3] = [p0,p1], reverse [p1,p0]. Desired i=0: p_{1}=p1; i=1: p_0=p0. Good. For k=0: w=[1,0,0], br=[0,0,1], conv=[0,p1,p0,0]? slice [1:3]=[p1,p0], reverse [p0,p1]. Desired i=0 p0, i=1 p_{-1}=0? Wait desired d_i=∑ p_l δ_{i+l=k}. For k=0: i=0 l=0 ->p0; i=1 l=-1 ->0. Our formula gave p1 at i=1. Wrong! Let's recalc. Desired d_i = p_{k-i} if 0≤k-i<n else 0. For k=0: d0=p0,d1=0. Our hankel_mul returned [p0,p1], wrong. Uh oh. Need fix hankel orientation.

Our formula `conv[n-1:2n-1][::-1]` derived for d_i=∑ p_l w_{i+l} with w length L=2n-1. For one-hot w_k, d_i=p_{k-i}. For k=0, i=1 -> p_{-1}=0. But conv gave p1. Let's re-derive formula. We defined d_i = ∑_{l=0}^{n-1} p_l w_{i+l}. This is for w indexed 0..L-1. For w one-hot at k, d_i = p_l where i+l=k -> l=k-i. If k-i in [0,n-1], yes. For k=0,i=1 l=-1 none ->0.
Our convolution formula: conv(a, br)[m] =∑_l a_l br[m-l]=∑ p_l w[L-1-(m-l)] (because br index = L-1 - (m-l) = L-1-m+l). We set L-1-m = i? Then term w[i+l]. We need m = L-1-i. For n=2,L=3. i=1 -> m=1. conv[1] for w0? w=[1,0,0], br=[0,0,1]. conv: m0 p0*0=0; m1 p0*br1(0)+p1*br0(0)=0? Wait p length2 indices 0,1; br length3. conv[1]=p0*br[1]+p1*br[0]=0+0=0. But earlier I said p1. Let's compute correctly: p=[p0,p1], br=[0,0,1]. Linear conv:
index0 p0*0=0
index1 p0*0 + p1*0=0
index2 p0*1 + p1*0 = p0
index3 p1*1 = p1
So conv=[0,0,p0,p1]. Slice conv[n-1:2n-1] = conv[1:3] = [0,p0]; reverse [p0,0]. Desired [p0,0]. Good. My previous mental wrong. For k=1, w=[0,1,0], br=[0,1,0]. conv: idx0 0; idx1 p0; idx2 p1; idx3 0? wait p0*br2(0)+p1*br1(1)=p1 at idx2. conv=[0,p0,p1,0]. slice [1:3]=[p0,p1], reverse [p1,p0]. Desired d0=p1,d1=p0. Good. So formula correct for k=0. Great.

Now, gradient of LSE: w from conv coefficients indexed 0..L-1. h_i =∑_l p_l w_{i+l}. For one-hot max lag k, h_i=p_{k-i}. Derivative g_i=2p_{k-i}. For k=0 (edge), derivative only i=0: 2p0. Good. So correct.

```python
"""
Search for non-negative sequences minimizing
    2*n*max(conv(a,a))/(sum(a))^2.

The solver works with probability vectors p (sum 1), so the objective is
    2*n*max(conv(p,p)).

Main ideas:
- Exact FFT convolution and exact gradient/Hankel multiplication.
- A custom local search that uses active top-lag weights to derive
  one-point "vertex" moves and two-point mass-transfer moves.
- Line search for those moves is done analytically using the affine form
  of the new convolution, without re-FFT for every trial step.
- Mirror-descent / log-noise / Fourier mutations for multi-basin exploration.
- Final L-BFGS polishing in the log-softmax domain on an LSE-smoothed
  surrogate, followed by pruning and sincere verification.
"""

import numpy as np
import time
from scipy.fft import fft, ifft
from scipy.optimize import minimize


def normalize_prob(vec):
    """Clip to non-negative and rescale to sum one."""
    v = np.asarray(vec, dtype=np.float64)
    if v.size == 0:
        return v
    v = np.nan_to_num(v, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    v = np.where(v > 0.0, v, 0.0)
    s = float(v.sum())
    if s < 1e-300:
        return np.full(v.shape, 1.0 / v.size, dtype=np.float64)
    return v / s


def conv_self(vec):
    """Full linear self-convolution via FFT."""
    a = np.asarray(vec, dtype=np.float64)
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.zeros(0, dtype=np.float64)
    size = 1 << (2 * n - 1).bit_length()
    A = fft(a, size)
    c = np.real(ifft(A * A))
    return c[:2 * n - 1]


def objective_prob(vec, c=None):
    """Objective for a probability vector: 2*n*max(conv(p,p))."""
    v = np.asarray(vec, dtype=np.float64)
    if c is None:
        c = conv_self(v)
    s = float(v.sum())
    if v.size == 0 or s < 1e-300:
        return np.inf
    m = float(np.max(c))
    if not np.isfinite(m):
        return np.inf
    if m < 0.0:
        m = 0.0
    return float(2.0 * v.size * m / (s * s))


def project_simplex(v, S):
    """Project v onto {x >= 0, sum(x)=S}."""
    n = len(v)
    if n == 0:
        return np.zeros(0, dtype=np.float64)
    v = np.asarray(v, dtype=np.float64)
    w = np.sort(v)[::-1]
    cum = np.cumsum(w)
    rho = np.arange(1, n + 1, dtype=np.float64)
    theta = (cum - S) / rho
    valid = theta <= w
    idx_true = np.where(valid)[0]
    if idx_true.size == 0:
        idx = n - 1
    else:
        idx = int(idx_true[-1])
    theta_use = float((cum[idx] - S) / (idx + 1))
    return np.maximum(v - theta_use, 0.0)


def hankel_mul(p, w):
    """
    Return (H_w p)_i = sum_l p_l * w_{i+l}, i=0..n-1, where
    w has length 2n-1.
    """
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    w = np.asarray(w, dtype=np.float64)
    n = p.size
    L = 2 * n - 1
    if w.size != L:
        tmp = np.zeros(L, dtype=np.float64)
        m = min(L, w.size)
        tmp[:m] = w[:m]
        w = tmp
    br = w[::-1]
    size = 1 << (L + n - 1).bit_length()
    P = fft(p, size)
    W = fft(br, size)
    convv = np.real(ifft(P * W))
    return convv[n - 1:2 * n - 1][::-1]


def top_lag_weights(c, K):
    """Uniform weights on the K largest convolution coefficients."""
    L = len(c)
    K = int(max(1, min(K, L)))
    if K == L:
        w = np.ones(L, dtype=np.float64)
        w /= L
        return w
    idx = np.argpartition(c, -K)[-K:]
    w = np.zeros(L, dtype=np.float64)
    w[idx] = 1.0 / K
    return w


def fourier_mutate_prob(p, rng, scale):
    """Fourier phase mutation, keeping the sum fixed."""
    p = normalize_prob(p)
    if scale <= 1e-15:
        return p.copy()
    n = p.size
    fsize = 1 << (2 * n - 1).bit_length()
    A = fft(p, fsize)
    mag = np.abs(A)
    phase = np.angle(A)
    freqs = np.arange(fsize, dtype=np.float64)
    noise = rng.normal(0.0, scale * (1.0 + freqs / max(1, fsize)), fsize)
    newA = mag * np.exp(1j * (phase + noise))
    newA[0] = A[0]
    newp = np.real(ifft(newA, fsize))[:n]
    return normalize_prob(project_simplex(newp, 1.0))


def random_sparse_prob(n, rng, alpha):
    """Sparse random start with support size about alpha*sqrt(n)."""
    n = int(n)
    if n <= 1:
        return np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) / max(1, n)
    m = int(round(alpha * np.sqrt(n)))
    m = max(2, min(n, m))
    js = rng.choice(n, size=m, replace=False)
    weights = rng.dirichlet(np.ones(m, dtype=np.float64))
    p = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    p[js] = weights
    return p


def upsample_prob(p_in, n_target):
    """Interpolate a probability vector to a new length."""
    p = normalize_prob(p_in)
    n_in = len(p)
    n_target = int(n_target)
    if n_in == n_target:
        return p.copy()
    if n_in <= 1 or n_target <= 1:
        return np.full(n_target, 1.0 / max(1, n_target), dtype=np.float64)
    x_old = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n_in)
    x_new = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n_target)
    f = np.interp(x_new, x_old, p * n_in)
    f = np.where(f > 0.0, f, 0.0)
    p_new = f / n_target
    return normalize_prob(p_new)


def softmax(x):
    """Stable softmax."""
    x = np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float64)
    if x.size == 0:
        return x.copy()
    x = x - np.max(x)
    e = np.exp(x)
    s = float(e.sum())
    if s < 1e-300:
        return np.full(x.shape, 1.0 / x.size, dtype=np.float64)
    return e / s


def lbfgs_fun(x, beta):
    """
    LSE surrogate loss and gradient in log-softmax variables.
    Loss: (1/beta) logsumexp(beta*conv(p,p)), p=softmax(x).
    """
    x = np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float64)
    beta = float(beta)
    if beta <= 0.0:
        beta = 1.0
    n = x.size
    if n == 0:
        return 0.0, np.zeros(0, dtype=np.float64)

    e = np.exp(x - np.max(x))
    s = float(e.sum())
    if s < 1e-300:
        return 1e100, np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    p = e / s

    c = conv_self(p)
    cm = float(np.max(c))
    if not np.isfinite(cm):
        return 1e100, np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)

    scaled = beta * (c - cm)
    scaled = np.minimum(scaled, 0.0)
    ws = np.exp(scaled)
    wsum = float(ws.sum())
    if wsum < 1e-300:
        w = np.zeros_like(c, dtype=np.float64)
        w[int(np.argmax(c))] = 1.0
    else:
        w = ws / wsum

    loss = cm + float(np.log(wsum)) / beta
    g = 2.0 * hankel_mul(p, w)
    pg = float(np.dot(p, g))
    g = g - pg * p

    if not np.all(np.isfinite(g)):
        return 1e100, np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    if not np.isfinite(loss):
        loss = 1e100
    return float(loss), g


def run_lbfgs_polish(p0, beta, maxiter=20, maxls=8):
    """Polish a probability vector by L-BFGS on log p."""
    p = normalize_prob(p0)
    n = p.size
    if n == 0:
        return p
    x = np.log(np.maximum(p, 1e-300))
    x = x - np.max(x)
    try:
        res = minimize(
            lbfgs_fun,
            x,
            method="L-BFGS-B",
            jac=True,
            args=(float(beta),),
            options={
                "maxiter": int(max(5, maxiter)),
                "maxls": int(max(4, maxls)),
                "ftol": 1e-14,
                "gtol": 1e-10,
                "maxcor": 12,
                "disp": False,
            },
        )
        p_new = softmax(res.x)
    except Exception:
        p_new = p.copy()
    return normalize_prob(p_new)


def add_candidate(ps, objs, p):
    """Top-level helper to append a candidate probability vector."""
    p = normalize_prob(p)
    objs.append(objective_prob(p))
    ps.append(p)


def prune_prob(p, eps):
    """Set tiny coordinates to zero and renormalize."""
    q = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    q = np.nan_to_num(q, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    q = np.where(q > 0.0, q, 0.0)
    q[q < float(eps)] = 0.0
    return normalize_prob(q)


def run_local_refine(p0, deadline, rng):
    """
    Active-lag local search with analytical two-point and vertex moves.
    """
    p = normalize_prob(p0)
    n = p.size
    if n == 0:
        return p, np.inf

    start = time.monotonic()
    if start >= deadline:
        c = conv_self(p)
        return p, objective_prob(p, c)

    c = conv_self(p)
    obj = objective_prob(p, c)
    best_p = p.copy()
    best_obj = obj

    L = 2 * n - 1
    delta_fr = np.array([0.005, 0.015, 0.04, 0.09, 0.18, 0.35, 0.65], dtype=np.float64)
    t_vert = np.array([0.005, 0.015, 0.03, 0.06, 0.12, 0.25, 0.50], dtype=np.float64)
    Klist = [1, 2, 4, 8, 16, 32, 64, 128, 256]

    Ubase_buf = np.empty(L, dtype=np.float64)
    v_buf = np.empty(L, dtype=np.float64)
    tmp_buf = np.empty(L, dtype=np.float64)

    it = 0
    stall = 0

    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        elapsed = time.monotonic() - start
        progress = min(1.0, elapsed / max(1.0, deadline - start))

        c = conv_self(p)
        obj = objective_prob(p, c)
        if obj < best_obj - 1e-13:
            best_obj = obj
            best_p = p.copy()
            stall = 0
        else:
            stall += 1

        if time.monotonic() > deadline - 0.05:
            break

        K = Klist[it % len(Klist)]
        w = top_lag_weights(c, K)
        h = hankel_mul(p, w)
        h = np.where(np.isfinite(h), h, 1.0)
        h = np.maximum(h, 0.0)

        if progress < 0.3:
            lc = 10
        elif progress < 0.7:
            lc = 18
        else:
            lc = 26
        lc = max(1, min(lc, n))
        if lc < n:
            low_js = np.argpartition(h, lc - 1)[:lc]
        else:
            low_js = np.arange(n)

        score = p * h
        if progress < 0.3:
            hc = 8
        elif progress < 0.7:
            hc = 14
        else:
            hc = 20
        hc = max(1, min(hc, n))
        if hc < n:
            high_js = np.argpartition(-score, hc - 1)[:hc]
        else:
            high_js = np.arange(n)

        if n > 0:
            high_js = np.unique(np.concatenate((
                high_js,
                np.array([np.argmax(p), np.argmax(h)], dtype=np.int64)
            )))
            low_js = np.unique(np.concatenate((
                low_js,
                np.array([np.argmin(h)], dtype=np.int64)
            )))
            low_js = low_js[:min(40, n)]
            high_js = high_js[:min(30, n)]

        best_move_obj = obj
        best_kind = 0
        best_i = -1
        best_j = -1
        best_d = 0.0
        best_tv = 0.0

        cand = np.unique(np.concatenate((low_js, high_js)))
        if cand.size > 0:
            shift_map = {}
            for idx in cand:
                ii = int(idx)
                arr = np.zeros(L, dtype=np.float64)
                arr[ii:ii + n] = p
                shift_map[ii] = arr

            # Two-point mass transfer moves p_i -> p_j.
            for jj in low_js:
                j = int(jj)
                Uj = shift_map.get(j)
                if Uj is None:
                    continue
                for ii in high_js:
                    i = int(ii)
                    if i == j:
                        continue
                    pi = float(p[i])
                    if pi <= 1e-15:
                        continue
                    Ui = shift_map.get(i)
                    if Ui is None:
                        continue

                    np.subtract(Uj, Ui, out=Ubase_buf)
                    for dfrac in delta_fr:
                        d = float(dfrac) * pi
                        if d <= 1e-18:
                            continue
                        scale = 2.0 * d
                        np.multiply(Ubase_buf, scale, out=v_buf)
                        np.add(c, v_buf, out=v_buf)

                        d2 = d * d
                        idx_a = 2 * j
                        idx_b = 2 * i
                        idx_q = i + j
                        v_buf[idx_a] += d2
                        v_buf[idx_b] += d2
                        v_buf[idx_q] -= 2.0 * d2

                        nov = 2.0 * n * float(v_buf.max())
                        if nov < best_move_obj - 1e-13:
                            best_move_obj = nov
                            best_kind = 1
                            best_i = i
                            best_j = j
                            best_d = d

                    if time.monotonic() > deadline - 0.2:
                        break
                if time.monotonic() > deadline - 0.2:
                    break

            # Vertex moves: (1-t)*p + t*e_j.
            if time.monotonic() < deadline - 0.2:
                vc = 6 if progress < 0.5 else 10
                for jj in low_js[:vc]:
                    j = int(jj)
                    Uj = shift_map.get(j)
                    if Uj is None:
                        continue
                    cgg = np.zeros(L, dtype=np.float64)
                    cgg[2 * j] = 1.0
                    for tv in t_vert:
                        tv = float(tv)
                        a1 = 1.0 - tv
                        np.multiply(c, a1 * a1, out=v_buf)
                        np.multiply(Uj, 2.0 * tv * a1, out=tmp_buf)
                        v_buf += tmp_buf
                        np.multiply(cgg, tv * tv, out=tmp_buf)
                        v_buf += tmp_buf
                        nov = 2.0 * n * float(v_buf.max())
                        if nov < best_move_obj - 1e-13:
                            best_move_obj = nov
                            best_kind = 2
                            best_j = j
                            best_tv = tv

            if best_kind == 1:
                d = best_d
                if d > p[best_i]:
                    d = p[best_i]
                p = p.copy()
                p[best_i] -= d
                p[best_j] += d
                p = normalize_prob(p)
                obj = best_move_obj
                stall = 0
            elif best_kind == 2:
                old = p.copy()
                p = (1.0 - best_tv) * old
                p[best_j] += best_tv
                p = normalize_prob(p)
                obj = best_move_obj
                stall = 0
            else:
                # Fallback: multiplicative mirror steps and random escapes.
                hmax = float(np.max(h)) if h.size else 1.0
                improved_fallback = False
                if hmax > 1e-300:
                    for eta in (0.1, 0.25, 0.5, 1.0, 2.0):
                        newp = p * np.exp(np.clip(-eta * h / hmax, -12.0, 12.0))
                        newp = normalize_prob(newp)
                        c_chk = conv_self(newp)
                        o_chk = objective_prob(newp, c_chk)
                        if o_chk < obj - 1e-14:
                            p = newp
                            obj = o_chk
                            c = c_chk
                            improved_fallback = True
                            stall = 0
                            break

                if not improved_fallback:
                    for sigma in (0.05, 0.15, 0.35):
                        newp = p * np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, sigma, n))
                        newp = normalize_prob(newp)
                        c_chk = conv_self(newp)
                        o_chk = objective_prob(newp, c_chk)
                        if o_chk < obj:
                            p = newp
                            obj = o_chk
                            c = c_chk
                            stall = 0
                            break

                    if stall >= 30:
                        base = best_p if rng.random() < 0.5 else p
                        scale = 0.04 + 0.10 * min(1.0, stall / 100.0)
                        p = fourier_mutate_prob(base, rng, scale)
                        c = conv_self(p)
                        obj = objective_prob(p, c)
                        stall = 0

        it += 1
        if it % 25 == 0:
            print("     local it=%d obj=%.8f best=%.8f" % (it, obj, best_obj))

    c_best = conv_self(best_p)
    ob = objective_prob(best_p, c_best)
    if ob < best_obj:
        best_obj = ob
    return best_p, best_obj


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400):
    """
    Execute the full search. Returns a list of non-negative floats.
    """
    total_start = time.monotonic()
    budget = float(budget_s) if budget_s is not None else 2400.0
    budget = max(60.0, min(budget, 2400.0))
    deadline = total_start + budget - 25.0

    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)

    # Load parent construction if available.
    p_parent = None
    try:
        arr = np.asarray(height_sequence_1, dtype=np.float64).reshape(-1)
        arr = np.nan_to_num(arr, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
        arr = np.where(arr > 0.0, arr, 0.0)
        if arr.size >= 4 and float(arr.sum()) > 0.0:
            p_parent = normalize_prob(arr)
    except Exception:
        p_parent = None

    # Keep a reasonable working length.
    if p_parent is None:
        x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, 8192)
        p_parent = normalize_prob(np.maximum(1.0 + 0.35 * np.sin(np.pi * x), 1e-3))
    else:
        if p_parent.size > 16384:
            p_parent = upsample_prob(p_parent, 8192)
        elif p_parent.size < 8192:
            p_parent = upsample_prob(p_parent, 8192)

    n = len(p_parent)
    best_p = p_parent.copy()
    best_obj = objective_prob(best_p)
    print("starting parent: n=%d obj=%.8f" % (n, best_obj))

    cand_ps = []
    cand_objs = []

    # Base transformations.
    add_candidate(cand_ps, cand_objs, p_parent)
    try:
        rev = p_parent[::-1].copy()
        add_candidate(cand_ps, cand_objs, rev)
        sym = 0.5 * (p_parent + rev)
        add_candidate(cand_ps, cand_objs, sym)
    except Exception:
        pass

    for gamma in (0.35, 0.5, 0.7, 1.0, 1.35, 1.8, 2.5):
        add_candidate(cand_ps, cand_objs, np.power(np.maximum(p_parent, 0.0), gamma))

    x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)
    for amp in (0.15, 0.35, 0.6):
        for phase in (0.0, np.pi / 2.0, np.pi, 1.5 * np.pi):
            w1 = np.maximum(1.0 + amp * np.cos(2.0 * np.pi * x + phase), 1e-4)
            add_candidate(cand_ps, cand_objs, p_parent * w1)
            w2 = np.maximum(1.0 - amp * np.cos(2.0 * np.pi * x + phase), 1e-4)
            add_candidate(cand_ps, cand_objs, p_parent * w2)

    for scale in (0.05, 0.12, 0.25, 0.5):
        add_candidate(cand_ps, cand_objs, fourier_mutate_prob(p_parent, rng, scale))

    for sig in (0.08, 0.25, 0.7):
        add_candidate(cand_ps, cand_objs, p_parent * np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, sig, n)))

    for alpha in (1.5, 2.5, 4.0, 7.0, 12.0):
        add_candidate(cand_ps, cand_objs, random_sparse_prob(n, rng, alpha))

    # Coarse-space starts: optimize small n, then upsample.
    if deadline - time.monotonic() > 800:
        for m in (256, 512, 1024):
            if n <= m:
                continue
            if time.monotonic() > deadline - 780:
                break
            pc = upsample_prob(p_parent, m)
            t_end = min(deadline - 760, time.monotonic() + 25)
            if t_end <= time.monotonic():
                break
            try:
                pc_opt, _ = run_local_refine(pc, t_end, rng)
                p_up = upsample_prob(pc_opt, n)
                add_candidate(cand_ps, cand_objs, p_up)
            except Exception:
                add_candidate(cand_ps, cand_objs, pc)

    if not cand_ps:
        cand_ps.append(p_parent.copy())
        cand_objs.append(best_obj)

    # Sort candidates by current objective.
    order = np.argsort(cand_objs)
    cand_ps = [cand_ps[i] for i in order]
    cand_objs = [cand_objs[i] for i in order]

    keep = min(18, len(cand_ps))
    cand_ps = cand_ps[:keep]
    cand_objs = cand_objs[:keep]

    if cand_objs[0] < best_obj:
        best_obj = cand_objs[0]
        best_p = cand_ps[0].copy()
    print("after candidate assembly: n=%d best=%.8f" % (n, best_obj))

    # Quick screening on a few full-size candidates.
    n_screen = min(10, len(cand_ps))
    for idx in range(n_screen):
        if time.monotonic() > deadline - 650:
            break
        t_alloc = 18.0
        if idx == 0:
            t_alloc = min(40.0, deadline - time.monotonic() - 650)
        else:
            t_alloc = min(20.0, deadline - time.monotonic() - 650)
        t_end = time.monotonic() + max(5.0, t_alloc)
        if t_end <= time.monotonic():
            break
        try:
            p_opt, obj_opt = run_local_refine(cand_ps[idx], t_end, rng)
            if obj_opt < best_obj:
                best_obj = obj_opt
                best_p = p_opt.copy()
            cand_ps[idx] = p_opt
            cand_objs[idx] = obj_opt
        except Exception:
            pass

    # Re-sort after screening.
    if len(cand_objs) > 1:
        order = np.argsort(cand_objs)
        cand_ps = [cand_ps[i] for i in order]
        cand_objs = [cand_objs[i] for i in order]
        keep2 = min(12, len(cand_ps))
        cand_ps = cand_ps[:keep2]
        cand_objs = cand_objs[:keep2]

    # Main deep local search.
    if deadline - time.monotonic() > 120:
        main_end = min(deadline - 380, time.monotonic() + 1500)
        if main_end > time.monotonic():
            try:
                p_main, obj_main = run_local_refine(best_p, main_end, rng)
                if obj_main < best_obj:
                    best_obj = obj_main
                    best_p = p_main.copy()
            except Exception:
                pass
    print("after main local search: best=%.8f" % best_obj)

    # L-BFGS polishing in log space.
    lbfgs_specs = [(8.0, 25, 430), (30.0, 22, 260), (120.0, 18, 130), (500.0, 14, 70)]
    for beta, maxiter, need in lbfgs_specs:
        if time.monotonic() >= deadline - need:
            continue
        if n > 12000:
            maxiter = max(8, int(0.6 * maxiter))
        try:
            p2 = run_lbfgs_polish(best_p, beta, maxiter=maxiter, maxls=10)
            o2 = objective_prob(p2)
            if o2 < best_obj:
                best_obj = o2
                best_p = p2.copy()

            for eps in (1e-9, 1e-7, 1e-5):
                p3 = prune_prob(best_p, eps)
                o3 = objective_prob(p3)
                if o3 < best_obj:
                    best_obj = o3
                    best_p = p3.copy()

            if time.monotonic() < deadline - max(30, need - 25):
                le = min(deadline - max(30, need - 25), time.monotonic() + 20)
                if le > time.monotonic():
                    p4, o4 = run_local_refine(best_p, le, rng)
                    if o4 < best_obj:
                        best_obj = o4
                        best_p = p4.copy()
        except Exception:
            pass

    # Final deterministic tests: reversal and symmetrization.
    for trial in (best_p[::-1].copy(), 0.5 * (best_p + best_p[::-1])):
        pt = normalize_prob(trial)
        ot = objective_prob(pt)
        if ot < best_obj:
            best_obj = ot
            best_p = pt.copy()

    # Pruning at several scales.
    for eps in (1e-10, 1e-8, 1e-6, 1e-4, 1e-2):
        pt = prune_prob(best_p, eps)
        ot = objective_prob(pt)
        if ot < best_obj:
            best_obj = ot
            best_p = pt.copy()

    # Optional larger-length attempt if still above target and time remains.
    if time.monotonic() < deadline - 430 and best_obj > 1.5027 and n < 16384:
        n2 = min(16384, 2 * n)
        try:
            p2 = upsample_prob(best_p, n2)
            end2 = min(deadline - 80, time.monotonic() + 320)
            if end2 > time.monotonic():
                p2o, o2o = run_local_refine(p2, end2, rng)
                if o2o < best_obj:
                    best_obj = o2o
                    best_p = p2o
            if time.monotonic() < deadline - 60:
                p2b = run_lbfgs_polish(best_p, 50.0, maxiter=12, maxls=8)
                o2b = objective_prob(p2b)
                if o2b < best_obj:
                    best_obj = o2b
                    best_p = p2b
        except Exception:
            pass

    # Final sanitize.
    best_p = np.asarray(best_p, dtype=np.float64)
    best_p = np.nan_to_num(best_p, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    best_p = np.where(best_p > 0.0, best_p, 0.0)
    best_p = np.clip(best_p, 0.0, 1000.0)
    best_p = normalize_prob(best_p)

    final_list = [float(x) for x in best_p]
    print("final candidate: n=%d direct_obj=%.8f" % (len(final_list), objective_prob(best_p)))

    # Verification if evaluate_sequence is available.
    verified = None
    try:
        verified = evaluate_sequence(final_list)
        print("evaluate_sequence verification: %.8f" % float(verified))
    except Exception:
        verified = None

    if verified is None or not np.isfinite(verified):
        # A conservative fallback that is always valid.
        fallback = np.ones(len(best_p), dtype=np.float64)
        fallback = normalize_prob(fallback)
        final_list = [float(x) for x in fallback]
        print("verification failed or missing; using uniform fallback")

    print("done in %.1fs" % (time.monotonic() - total_start))
    return final_list
```
